---
name: code-review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** — does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** — does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you — run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point — a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here — not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) — fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

On top of whatever the repo documents, the Standards axis always carries the **smell baseline** below — a fixed set of Fowler code smells (_Refactoring_, ch.3) that applies even when a repo documents nothing. Two rules bind it:

- **The repo overrides.** A documented repo standard always wins; where it endorses something the baseline would flag, suppress the smell.
- **Always a judgement call.** Each smell is a labelled heuristic ("possible Feature Envy"), never a hard violation — and, like any standard here, skip anything tooling already enforces.

Each smell reads *what it is* → *how to fix*; match it against the diff:

- **Mysterious Name** — a function, variable, or type whose name doesn't reveal what it does or holds. → rename it; if no honest name comes, the design's murky.
- **Duplicated Code** — the same logic shape appears in more than one hunk or file in the change. → extract the shared shape, call it from both.
- **Feature Envy** — a method that reaches into another object's data more than its own. → move the method onto the data it envies.
- **Data Clumps** — the same few fields or params keep travelling together (a type wanting to be born). → bundle them into one type, pass that.
- **Primitive Obsession** — a primitive or string standing in for a domain concept that deserves its own type. → give the concept its own small type.
- **Repeated Switches** — the same `switch`/`if`-cascade on the same type recurs across the change. → replace with polymorphism, or one map both sites share.
- **Shotgun Surgery** — one logical change forces scattered edits across many files in the diff. → gather what changes together into one module.
- **Divergent Change** — one file or module is edited for several unrelated reasons. → split so each module changes for one reason.
- **Speculative Generality** — abstraction, parameters, or hooks added for needs the spec doesn't have. → delete it; inline back until a real need shows.
- **Message Chains** — long `a.b().c().d()` navigation the caller shouldn't depend on. → hide the walk behind one method on the first object.
- **Middle Man** — a class or function that mostly just delegates onward. → cut it, call the real target direct.
- **Refused Bequest** — a subclass or implementer that ignores or overrides most of what it inherits. → drop the inheritance, use composition.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3, **plus the smell baseline from step 3** pasted in full — the sub-agent has no other access to it.
- The brief: "Report — per file/hunk where relevant — (a) every place the diff violates a documented standard: cite the standard (file + the rule); and (b) any baseline smell you spot: name it and quote the hunk. Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls — documented-standard breaches can be hard, but baseline smells are always judgement calls, and a documented repo standard overrides the baseline. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings — the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes — that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

+  /** G-NOISE: delta_c at most 1/noiseDivisor of the limiting contrast's median effect at the qualifying dose. */
+  noiseDivisor: 2,
+  /** Frozen wording of the power statement (verified exactly in tests; never computed with floats in the payload). */
+  powerStatement:
+    "The first Holm rejection needs at least 13 of 16 blocks positive (p = 697/65,536). A contrast whose true per-block positive probability is 0.75 passes it about 40.5% of the time, 0.85 about 79%, 0.90 about 93%, 0.95 about 99%. A negative therefore means no benefit of size at least delta in at least about 90% of blocks, not no benefit.",
+};
+export type ProtocolFe = typeof PROTOCOL_FE;
+
+/** Every seed that can run in this rung: block, replicate (primary and noise-control offsets), wall, fixture. Sorted, unique. */
+export function enumerateSeedsFe(p: ProtocolFe = PROTOCOL_FE): number[] {
+  const out = new Set<number>();
+  const offs = (n: number, s: number) => {
+    for (let k = 0; k < n; k++) out.add(s + k * p.replicateStride);
+  };
+  for (const s of [...p.devSeeds, ...p.freshSeeds, ...p.wallSeeds]) offs(p.R, s);
+  for (const s of p.fixtureSeeds) offs(p.fixtureR, s);
+  for (const s of p.devReplicateSeeds) offs(2 * p.R, s);
+  return [...out].sort((a, b) => a - b);
+}
+
+/** SHA-256 of the canonical second-rung protocol, the registry digest it was checked against, and the enumerated seed list. */
+export function protocolHashFe(registryDigest: string, p: ProtocolFe = PROTOCOL_FE): string {
+  return sha256HexJS(new TextEncoder().encode(canonicalJSON({ protocol: p, registryDigest, seeds: enumerateSeedsFe(p) })));
+}

     1	# I06b Free-energy-matched comparators: niche construction, second rung (slice plan v2, after Codex plan review)
     2	
     3	Status: PLAN stage, 2026-11-02. Branch `claude/i06-free-energy-matched` from `origin/main` 0bdd7a70. Sources: "I06" in `docs/simulation-research-2026-11-01/report.md`; the first rung `docs/niche-construction-2026-11-01/` (PLAN, EVIDENCE, REVIEW, sealed `evidence.json`); coordinator decisions D1, D3 in `/Users/nicholas/develop/browser-life-run-1102-log/docs/backlog-run-2026-11-02.html`. Exploratory, CPU only (`sim-ref`; GPU is allowed by the scope but not needed), mutation off, no physics change, no model calls, no deploy. v2 revisions answer `REVIEW.md` "Plan review dispositions" (P1-1..5, P2-6..14). This is a rung on the same tool, not a fork: every new behaviour is keyed off a second protocol object so the first rung's sealed evidence still verifies byte for byte.
     4	
     5	## 1. Bounded question
     6	
     7	Rung 1 ended `unsupported-measurement` for all three builder candidates (clusters 0, 4, 9). Three defects, each named in `EVIDENCE.md`:
     8	
     9	1. **Comparator confound.** Receiver-alone controls (d0, d1E, d1B) match *total* energy but not *free* energy E (cluster 0, seed 8600501: 9,917 vs 22,169, both totals 26,969). The negative inoculum contrasts (23 of 24 blocks) therefore cannot be blamed on inoculum alone.
    10	2. **Noise floor above the effect.** Dev replicate floors delta = 341 / 291 / 404 against builder polymer of 103.5 / 98 / 84.5 and a receiver reserve of B_r = 480.
    11	3. **No positive control at realistic scale.** The hand wall was detected only at 1200 polymer (about 10x what builders leave); at 120 it was not (W1 4/8, W2 3/8 on dev).
    12	
    13	Question for this rung: when the receiver-alone benchmark is matched on free energy as well as on total energy and matter, and the readout is powered to a noise floor below the effect it must detect, (a) does the assay pass a positive control at a stated, realistic polymer dose, and, only if so, (b) does a builder's conditioned field causally raise a receiver's receiver-labelled biomass inventory (survival is a viability requirement on the field arm, not a claimed benefit; a paired survival contrast is reported as attribution only) relative to nonbuilder, ablation, scramble and the free-energy-matched receiver-alone controls?
    14	
    15	Valid outcomes, all reported as such: `supported` for a pair; `unsupported-within-tested-domain` (a usable negative, only when the positive control passed); `unsupported-measurement` with a stated minimum detectable polymer dose (the "stated reason none exists" branch of the scope); `invalid-or-incomplete`. A negative here does not say a second link is impossible, does not test mutation reaching builder or receiver, and does not test A+B->C.
    16	
    17	## 2. What is built (one PR)
    18	
    19	1. **Evidence fixtures with a negative case that fools the old inoculum contrast** (section 4.5). New fixture `far-field-energy`: a conditioned field that differs from its controls only by extra free energy laid in a band outside the inoculum footprint plus halo (no polymer). Because ablation only converts P to C (`transfer.ts`), with no polymer `b` and `ab` stay identical and `D_poly = 0`, so the complete rung-1 gate and the new gate both reject it. What the fixture tests is the **inoculum contrast alone**: rung-1 `D_inoc` (vs d0/d1E/d1B/region-matched) reads positive (fooled) while `D_inoc2` (with fU/fS/fB) does not. Evidence records `fullGateLabel` (unsupported, by `D_poly`) and `inocFooledOld` / `inocCaughtNew` separately; the claim made is about the contrast, never "the old rule would label it supported". A full-gate trap needs a polymer-bearing confound and is deferred (section 11). If the fixture cannot fool `D_inoc` at unit scale, the test fails and the slice reports that instead of weakening the claim.
    20	2. **Small CPU path, same library.** Extensions in `packages/runner/src/niche/`: second protocol (`PROTOCOL_FE`), free-energy-matched comparators fU, fS, fB, R-replicate Stage-2 readout, dose-ladder positive control, decision with `inoc2`. `tools/niche/niche.ts` gains `--rung fe` (selects the second protocol and docs directory) in front of every existing mode; no new CLI.
    21	3. **Manifest and machine-readable evidence:** `docs/niche-construction-fe-2026-11-02/{evidence.json,manifest.json,calibration.json,protocol.lock.json}`, sealed like rung 1 with `--write`, `--check`, `--replay-check`.
    22	4. **Local page.** `apps/lab/niche/index.html` gains a "Second rung: free-energy-matched" section (same private route, still 404 on the deployed site) reading the second `evidence.json`: free-energy table per comparator (the 9,917 vs 22,169 style numbers, before and after matching), noise floor vs effect bar, dose-ladder positive-control chart, edge per candidate, the `far-field-energy` panel. Reads evidence only, runs no simulation.
    23	5. Docs: this PLAN (carries the dated amendment, section 10), `REVIEW.md` (Codex dispositions), `EVIDENCE.md` (human result, written after the run).

    25	## 3. Reuse and honest limits of reuse
    26	
    27	- Reuse as is: `transfer.ts` (ledger, relabel, placement, scramble), `arms.ts` builders, `assay.ts` stage runner and readouts, `decision.ts` sign test / intersection-union / Holm, `evidence.ts` sealing, the rung-1 page helpers (`render.ts`), `EVIDENCE_STATUSES`. Rung-1 `PROTOCOL` object and every rung-1 code path stay byte-identical in behaviour; `--check` and `--replay-check` for rung 1 are acceptance gates (G7).
    28	- **Registry binding.** Rung 1 hashes the whole registry (minus owners listed in `LATER_SLICE_OWNERS`). This rung adds the owner prefix `i06b free-energy-matched` to that list (the I08 precedent) and binds its own digest over its own reservation only, so neither rung's evidence is invalidated by the other's reservation.
    29	- **Audit.** `transfer.ts` `Audit`/`residuals` have no credit term and `runArm` audits every census. This rung adds **no credit to the audit**: each arm's ledger baseline is that arm's own constructed starting total (lattice + reserve, heatAnalytic 0), so each arm's physical residual is exactly `0n` as in rung 1. The construction difference between `b` and a comparator is a comparison-budget `constructionGap` recorded in evidence only, not a ledger entry (it is not energy created inside any run). If the existing audit cannot take a per-arm baseline, add an optional `baseline` parameter defaulting to current behaviour and prove rung-1 serialization unchanged by test.
    30	- **Manifest conflict (G7).** `tools/niche/niche.ts` `sourceFiles` hashes every niche `.ts` and `checkSaved` rejects changed or added files, so editing existing niche sources or adding modules fails rung-1 `--check` unless the rung-1 `manifest.json` is re-pinned. As in the I08 precedent (rung-1 REVIEW dated note 2026-11-02), a **manifest-only re-pin** of `docs/niche-construction-2026-11-01/manifest.json` is allowed; `evidence.json`, `calibration.json`, `protocol.lock.json`, PLAN, EVIDENCE and REVIEW of rung 1 stay byte-identical to `origin/main`, and rung-1 `--check` and `--replay-check` must pass after the re-pin. New rung-fe code goes in new files wherever possible to keep the diff to existing sources small.
    31	- No change to `packages/schema`, `packages/sim-ref`, `packages/sim-gpu`, `defaultConfig()`, `RULE_VERSION`, golden pins, vendor, preregistration.
    32	- All new arithmetic is analytical, integer (BigInt where it touches totals), outside the step function.
    33	
    34	## 4. Design
    35	
    36	Everything in rung-1 PLAN section 4 holds unless stated: single 24x24 tile, kernelRadius 5, mutation off, ladder genes, recurring injury with shell, T1 = 60, T2 = 80, reserve R = (480, 960), footprint (14,12) r3, halo 2, absolute clock T1 for every Stage-2 arm, candidates by panel `{0, 4, 9}` and receiver cluster 12, drop order 9 then 4 by runtime only.
    37	
    38	### 4.1 Free-energy-matched comparators (answers defect 1)
    39	
    40	Free energy means lattice channel E (`sum E`), reported next to total energy (`totalsOf`), for the builder-removed arm `b` and each comparator, at the start of Stage 2 (before inoculum placement) and after placement. Matter is identical in every arm (lattice plus reserve), as in rung 1. Observed rung-1 direction (seed 8600501, cluster 0): the receiver-alone control d0 held MORE free E than `b` (22,169 vs 9,917) with equal total (26,969), because `b` holds its energy as bound matter.
    41	
    42	Matching free energy and total energy simultaneously is impossible at equal matter when the comparator has no conditioned composition (bound matter carries energy at eB, eP, eC, eA). Each comparator is a separately constructed initial state (not a transformation of `b` inside a run); its own ledger starts from its own total and conserves exactly. The difference is reported as `constructionGap = totalEnergy(b) - totalEnergy(f)` (signed BigInt, evidence only, never in the ledger): **gap > 0** means the comparator holds less total energy than the field arm; **gap < 0** means it holds more (a stronger comparator, the conservative side for the claim).
    43	
    44	- **fU (uniform free-matched):** receiver-alone lattice, uniform A, free E set uniform to `sum E(b)` over the lattice.
    45	- **fS (spatial free-matched):** as fU but E copied cell for cell from `b` (same free-energy field, no polymer, no biomass, matter all A in the remainder). Isolates "the energy pattern alone" from "structure".
    46	- **fB (free-matched plus biomass-matched):** fS plus the rung-1 d1B raise of B (region plus halo, matter converted from A), **without** the `(eB - eA)*dB` lattice debit, so free E stays equal to `b` cell for cell before and after placement; the bound-energy increase is entered as extra total energy (gap < 0, flagged `comparator-excess`). Test: free-E equality before and after placement.
    47	- Rung-1 d0, d1E, d1B are kept unchanged and reported (total-matched family).
    48	
    49	New inoculum contrast: `D_inoc2 = Y(b | builder) - max(Y(d1E), Y(d1B), Y(fU), Y(fS), Y(fB))`; every constructed comparator is in the max (none excluded by sign of gap; `comparator-excess` and `comparator-deficit` are labels in evidence). Rung-1 `D_inoc` is still computed and reported next to it. The four gating contrasts are builder, poly, space, inoc2. A comparator whose construction would need negative lattice E or exceed `POOL_MAX`/`MATTER_MAX` is refused (`credit-overdraw`, never clamped); a block where fU, fS and fB are all refused is `invalid-or-incomplete` for that candidate (reported, never dropped).
    50	
    51	### 4.2 Better-powered readout (answers defect 2)
    52	
    53	- **Replicated Stage 2.** Each arm's Stage 2 is run for `R = 4` sim seeds (block seed plus fixed offsets `{0, 1, 2, 3} * 1000`), identical offsets in every arm (so injury steps and RNG keys still coincide across arms); Stage 1 histories and transfers are shared. Primary endpoint `Y = sum over the R replicates of receiver-labelled B at T2` (integer; no division, units are x R of rung 1). Viability: receiver B above zero in at least `R - 1` of R replicates counts the field arm `b` as viable for that block. Viability is a requirement for the gate (field arm viable in at least 14 of 16 blocks; at a true per-block rate of 0.9, 15/16 would pass only 51% of the time). A paired survival contrast (arm vs comparator) is reported as attribution, never gating, and no survival benefit is claimed.
    54	- **Replicate seeds** are enumerated and reserved explicitly (5.1) for the maximum R of 8.
    55	- **More blocks:** 16 fresh blocks per candidate (was 8). Exact sign test over 16; `k = 16` gives 1/65,536, `k = 14` gives 137/65,536, `k = 12` gives 2,517/65,536 (hand-enumerated in tests). Candidate threshold by Holm at alpha 0.05 over 3 candidates as before (1/60, 1/40, 1/20 for the three ordered p-values).
    56	- **Power statement (frozen wording).** The first Holm rejection needs k >= 13 of 16 (p = 697/65,536). A contrast whose true per-block positive probability is 0.75 passes it only 40.5% of the time, 0.85 gives 79%, 0.90 gives 93%, 0.95 gives 99%. The design therefore detects consistent benefits (about 0.9 or higher of blocks positive and median above delta); a negative means "no benefit of size at least delta in at least about 90% of blocks", not "no benefit". Four contrasts, delta and viability can only lower the pass rate. Dev reports the empirical positive-block rate of the wall control at the qualifying dose next to this table.
    57	- **Delta from a replicate control with the same readout:** `delta = max(ceil(0.02 * B_r * R), 1.5 * max|D|)` over dev replicate pairs (nonbuilder vs nonbuilder, builder vs builder, on a disjoint replicate seed set of R sims). With the sum over R the expected floor falls by about sqrt(R) relative to rung 1 after rescaling; dev says whether it did.
    58	- Secondary readout (reported, never gating): per-replicate area under the receiver-B census curve (sum over the 8 censuses), as a lower-variance cross-check.
    59	- **Noise gate (G-NOISE, dev only, before any fresh seed), per candidate:** `delta_c` must be at most half of the wall control's median effect on the **limiting gating contrast** (the one with the smallest median effect among the four), in summed-R units, at that candidate's qualifying dose (4.3). Otherwise the rung stops `unsupported-measurement` and reports the achieved floor and the minimum detectable dose; the fresh seeds are not read.
    60	
    61	### 4.3 Positive control at realistic scale (answers defect 3)
    62	
    63	The hand-wall fixtures (W1 ring, W2 floor under the receiver; rung-1 definitions) run the full four-contrast gate on dev wall seeds across a dose ladder of total polymer `P in {60, 120, 240, 600, 1200}`, ascending, stopping each wall at its first passing dose (that dose is its minimum detectable dose); doses above the ceiling are run only if nothing at or below it passes, to state the minimum detectable dose.
    64	
    65	- **Per-candidate realistic ceiling** `C_c = 2 x` the median dev builder polymer at T1 of candidate c (rung 1 measured 84.5-103.5, so C is about 170-207, which admits 60 and 120, not 240). The control for candidate c uses doses `<= C_c` only.
    66	- **Ordinary placement required.** The rung-1 `wallGateBlock` uses `allowP: true` (overwriting polymer on injured cells), which real transfers (`placeAll` default false) forbid. The qualifying control must place the wall by the ordinary placement rule (no overwrite of existing polymer, same pool-cap and reserve guards) at cells where that is legal; wall variants that need `allowP: true` stay as a reported diagnostic and never qualify the assay. If no ordinary-placement wall geometry exists, the control is `unavailable` and the stated reason is that none exists, ending the rung `unsupported-measurement`.
    67	- **Wall pass rule (dev, 8 wall seeds):** for each of the four gating contrasts, at least 7 of 8 seeds positive (exact p = 9/256) and median >= `delta_c`. A wall passes at dose P iff all four hold. The control **qualifies candidate c** iff W1 or W2 passes at some dose `<= C_c` and G-NOISE holds (4.2).
    68	- A candidate that does not qualify ends `unsupported-measurement` with the minimum detectable dose, its ratio to the builder polymer and the floor that explains it; its fresh blocks are not simulated (a sealed stopped payload is still written, section 8). Candidates qualify independently.
    69	
    70	Dropped from this rung (cut for scope, section 11): the builder founder-biomass dose-response knob.
    71	
    72	### 4.4 Readouts, ledger and flux (unchanged from rung 1, per replicate)
    73	
    74	Per arm and replicate: receiver survival, receiver-labelled biomass inventory, flux attribution (synthesis share; "growth" only at share at least half and no saturated counter), builder cost, before/after inventories. Ledger invariant per arm: lattice + reserve + cumulative heatAnalytic against that arm's own starting total, exact `0n` at every census of both stages, including every replicate (the unchanged rung-1 audit; no credit term). Evidence adds per arm: free E, total energy, `constructionGap` vs `b`.
    75	
    76	### 4.5 Fixtures
    77	
    78	16x16, kernelRadius 5 at unit scale (rung-1 constraint); the positive control is additionally qualified at the frozen scale on dev.
    79	- `far-field-energy` (new, 2.1: fools rung-1 `D_inoc`, not `D_inoc2`; full gate rejects via `D_poly = 0`); also a uniform and an in-footprint variant, `caughtBy` recorded.
    80	- `free-gap-replay`: rung-1 observed direction at fixture scale (d0 holds MORE free E than `b` with equal totals; bound inventories as recorded); asserts fU/fS/fB reproduce `b`'s free E exactly (difference zero), that d0's excess is removed, and that `constructionGap` is recorded with every arm's own ledger residual `0n`.
    81	- `takeover-only`, `null-field`, `hand-wall-gate-W2` (rung-1 fixtures re-run with the second protocol; expectations unchanged).
    82	- Guards re-run: overwrite, pool cap, underfunded reserve, exhausted reserve, plus `credit-overdraw` (a free-matched comparator that would need negative lattice E or exceed a cap is refused, not clamped).
    83	
    84	## 5. Pre-stated rules (frozen before any fresh-seed run; dated amendment in section 10)
    85	
    86	### 5.1 Seeds
    87	Block seeds in `8,601,001-8,601,999` and every derived replicate seed are reserved in `experiments/discovery/seed-registry.json` (owner `i06b free-energy-matched`) **before** anything runs. Replicate sims use block seed + `k * 1000`, `k = 0..7` (R = 4 uses k = 0..3; R = 8 uses k = 0..7), so derived seeds reach 8,608,999: the reservation lists the contiguous range `8,601,001-8,608,999`; the implementer first runs `seedCollisions` over it and, if any existing owner collides, replaces the offset by a collision-free stride and records that in the amendment. The protocol hash binds the explicit enumerated list of every seed that can run (block, replicate, noise, wall, fixture) and the registry digest; `--check` rescans the enumerated list, not only base lists. Never reused: 8,600,101-108, 8,600,501-508, nor any other rung-1 seed. Dev blocks 8,601,001-8,601,008, dev replicate-noise 8,601,201-8,601,204, wall 8,601,301-8,601,308, fixtures 8,601,401-8,601,408, fresh 8,601,501-8,601,516. Dev outcomes may set only `T1, T2` (unchanged unless dev shows cause), `delta_c`, the dose rungs and ceilings, and `R` (4 or 8; one escalation to 8 allowed if the noise gate fails, recorded before the freeze). Fresh seeds are read once, after the freeze commit is pushed and verified (section 9).
    88	
    89	### 5.2 Estimands
    90	Primary `Y` as in 4.2, paired within a block. Gating contrasts: `D_builder`, `D_poly`, `D_space`, `D_inoc2`. Attribution only: `D_spaceP`, `D_spaceNP`, rung-1 `D_inoc`, `D_naive = Y(b) - Y(d0)`.
    91	
    92	### 5.3 Edge label
    93	Rung-1 rules with these changes only: 16 blocks; `inoc2` replaces `inoc`; per-contrast exact one-sided sign test (zeros non-positive) with `median >= delta`; field arm viable in at least 14 of 16 blocks (viability requirement, no survival-benefit claim); candidate p = max over four; Holm across candidates that ran. Adequacy is per candidate: the ordinary-placement positive control qualified that candidate at a dose within its ceiling and G-NOISE held (4.3); the rung-1 polymer criterion is a reported field only. `unsupported-within-tested-domain` only when valid, adequately measured and a gating test fails; its meaning is bounded by the power statement in 4.2. Mechanism text and the three-valued panel verdict as rung 1.
    94

   112	## 7. Tests (named cases)
   113	
   114	`niche-fe-comparators.test.ts`: `fU free E equals b free E exactly and matter equals`; `fS E channel equals b E channel cell for cell`; `fB free E equals b cell for cell before and after placement (no lattice debit, gap < 0 flagged comparator-excess)`; `constructionGap sign: positive when comparator total is lower`; `every arm residual 0n against its own baseline`; `rung-1 audit serialization unchanged`; `credit-overdraw refused not clamped`; `d0 d1E d1B unchanged from rung 1 (state hashes equal)`; `placement set still the intersection across arms`; `every arm starts at absolute step T1 with equal injury steps and RNG keys`.
   115	`niche-fe-readout.test.ts`: `replicate offsets identical in every arm`; `Y is the integer sum of R replicate endpoints`; `R=4 sum noise floor on a no-op control is below R=1 floor scaled by R (dev-scale fixture)`; `instrumented twin equals plain twin (stateHash) per replicate`; `deterministic replay: same block twice equal bytes`; `segmented stage 2 equals continuous`; `survival rule R-1 of R`; `AUC reported not gating`.
   116	`niche-fe-dose.test.ts`: `ceiling derives from dev builder P per candidate`; `no dose above the ceiling can qualify`; `allowP wall never qualifies, ordinary-placement wall required`; `wall pass rule 7 of 8 and median >= delta`; `failure yields minimum detectable dose and a sealed stopped payload`; `dose ladder deterministic across reruns`.
   117	`niche-fe-fixtures.test.ts`: `far-field-energy: naive positive, rung-1 D_inoc positive (fooled), D_inoc2 not positive, full gate unsupported via D_poly = 0 (caughtBy recorded)`; `free-gap-replay closes the free-E gap`; `takeover-only still zero synthesis`; `null-field exact zeros`; `hand-wall-gate-W2 supported`; every guard refuses.
   118	`niche-decision` additions in `niche-fe-readout`: exact sign p for n=16, k=12,14,16 against hand enumeration; Holm resolution at n=16; `no floats in sealed payload`; `panel verdict not negative when positive control failed`; `paired survival contrast reported, not gating`; `power table values (0.75: 40.5%, 0.9: 93.2%)`.
   119	`niche-fe-evidence.test.ts` (artifact tests run only after generation): `evidence.json sealed and verifies`; `manifest hashes match sources`; `--write and --replay-check refuse without a verifying lock and a pushed identical lock (guard test with a stubbed git)`; `fresh seeds and every derived replicate seed disjoint from 8600101-108, 8600501-508 and the live registry`; `stopped payload seals and verifies`; `rung-1 evidence still verifies after the manifest re-pin; rung-1 evidence/calibration/lock bytes equal origin/main`; `--check does not simulate`.
   120	`niche-fe-render.test.ts`: free-energy table rows incl. constructionGap; noise-vs-effect bar; stopped payload (positive control failed) shows "no fresh run" not "negative"; edge supported/unsupported/unavailable; caption "exploratory, CPU only"; `noindex` unchanged.
   121	
   122	## 8. Acceptance gates (runnable)
   123	
   124	```bash
   125	export PATH=~/.nvm/versions/node/v24.2.0/bin:$PATH
   126	cd /Users/nicholas/develop/browser-life-i06-free-energy-matched
   127	pnpm install
   128	pnpm typecheck
   129	pnpm vitest run --maxWorkers=2 packages/runner/test/niche-*.test.ts tools/test/niche-*.test.ts apps/lab/test/niche-*.test.ts
   130	node tools/niche/niche.ts --rung fe --bench                 # step rate, informational (pace if load > 40)
   131	node tools/niche/niche.ts --rung fe --calibrate             # dev + wall + dose ladder only; writes calibration.json
   132	node tools/niche/niche.ts --rung fe --freeze                # lock written; then the freeze commit and push (section 9)
   133	node tools/niche/niche.ts --rung fe --write                 # once; ALWAYS seals a payload (stopped payload if no candidate qualifies; fresh seeds simulated only for qualifying candidates)
   134	node tools/niche/niche.ts --rung fe --check
   135	node tools/niche/niche.ts --rung fe --replay-check
   136	node tools/niche/niche.ts --check && node tools/niche/niche.ts --replay-check   # rung 1 still byte-identical (G7)
   137	pnpm vitest run --maxWorkers=2 packages/sim-ref/test/golden-hashes.test.ts
   138	git diff --exit-code origin/main -- packages/schema packages/sim-ref packages/sim-gpu vendor experiments/preregistration.md experiments/FROZEN docs/rules.md
   139	git diff --name-only origin/main -- docs/niche-construction-2026-11-01   # must list only manifest.json
   140	```
   141	Gates: G1 comparator and ledger tests green, exact zero residual per arm and replicate; G2 fixtures behave as stated (far-field-energy fools `D_inoc` and not `D_inoc2`); G3 per candidate, ordinary-placement dev positive control passes at a dose within its ceiling, else that candidate stops with the minimum detectable dose and no fresh run; G-NOISE per candidate `delta_c` at most half the limiting-contrast control effect; G4 freeze (hash, seeds, amendment, source hashes) committed and pushed before any fresh seed is read, enforced in code (section 9); G5 `--check` and `--replay-check` pass; G6 regeneration budget (full workload, benchmarked with `--bench` before freeze; 3.7 ms/step unloaded): per block `2*T1 + 13 arms * R * T2` = 4,280 steps at R = 4 (about 16 s). Dev candidates 8 blocks x 3 = 24 blocks (about 6.5 min); replicate-noise ensemble about 1 min; wall ladder with stop-at-first-pass, 2 walls x up to 3 doses (60, 120, 240 as the ceiling needs) x 8 seeds x 12 arms x R x 80 = about 184,000 steps worst case (about 11 min; fewer on early passes); fixtures about 1 min; fresh 16 x 3 = 48 blocks (about 13 min). Calibration is run once and reused: `--write` loads the frozen `calibration.json` (verified against the lock) and does not re-derive it. Sum about 35-40 min unloaded, about 80 min at 2x load, plus replay-check (about 13 min); target under 2 hours including reviews, pace if load > 40 (it is above 60 at plan time), and cut scope in this order if the benchmark overruns: page extras, fixture variants, W1 wall; never the comparators, the control or the freeze; G7 rung-1 evidence, calibration and lock byte-identical; only its manifest re-pinned; rung-1 `--check` and `--replay-check` pass; G8 page screenshot spec passes at both widths and the PNGs are read.
   142	
   143	## 9. Freeze-before-run sequence (IMPLEMENT stage)
   144	
   145	1. Implement, unit tests green, dev `--calibrate`; fix the dev-set free parameters (`delta_c`, ceilings and dose rungs, R).
   146	2. Write the dated amendment (section 10, filled with the frozen numbers), `--freeze` to write `protocol.lock.json`, Codex Sol 6.1 review of the freeze (design, seeds, rules), address findings.
   147	3. Commit only the freeze set (code, PLAN, amendment, registry, `calibration.json`, `protocol.lock.json`; no `evidence.json`, `manifest.json`, `EVIDENCE.md`, page data), push `claude/i06-free-energy-matched`, no PR.
   148	4. **Enforced guard.** The rung-fe lock binds protocol, calibration, enumerated seeds, registry digest **and the hashes of every niche source file**. `--write` and `--replay-check` (every path that can simulate fresh seeds) first verify: the lock verifies against current protocol, calibration and sources; the frozen files in the working tree are clean against HEAD; and `git ls-tree origin/claude/i06-free-energy-matched` shows the same lock and frozen sources (pushed freeze). Otherwise they refuse before reading any fresh seed. A guard test stubs git for refuse and allow cases. The lock cannot name its own commit; the pushed-identical check is the ordering proof.
   149	5. Run `--write` (fresh seeds; seals a stopped payload instead if no candidate qualifies), `--check`, `--replay-check`, page and screenshots, write `EVIDENCE.md` from the evidence. Later fixes never edit the frozen rules; changes after the fresh read are recorded as a further amendment and the fresh seeds are then spent.
   150	
   151	## 10. Amendment 3 (dated 2026-11-02; written at freeze, before any fresh seed was read)
   152	
   153	Rung-1 amendments 1 and 2 are the history this builds on (`docs/niche-construction-2026-11-01/PLAN.md` section 10). Dev calibration was run once (`calibration.json`, log `calibrate.log`) on dev seeds 8601001-008, replicate-noise 8601201-204 and wall seeds 8601301-308. What it fixed, and what changed relative to sections 4 and 5:
   154	
   155	- **R = 4**, replicate stride 1000. The replicate-noise control compares replicate set A (offsets 0-3) with a disjoint set B (offsets 4-7) for the builder-removed and the nonbuilder-removed arm, so R is capped at 4 inside the reserved offsets; R = 8 would need a wider registry reservation first. The one allowed escalation to R = 8 was not taken: the wall effects on dev (below) are 10-40 times smaller than the measured floor and zero on most contrasts, so a factor of about sqrt(2) in noise cannot close them.
   156	- **delta per candidate (summed-R units)**: cluster 0: 1152, cluster 4: 1031, cluster 9: 1169 (`max(ceil(B_r * R / 50), ceil(1.5 * max|D|))` over 8 replicate pairs per candidate). Rung 1 had 341 / 291 / 404 for a single sim; divided by R the new floors are 288 / 258 / 292, so the sum of four sims lowered the floor relative to the effect by only about 15 to 35 percent, not enough.
   157	- **Realistic ceilings** (2 x the median dev builder polymer at T1, the twice-median integer): cluster 0: 200, cluster 4: 204, cluster 9: 169. The ladder 60, 120, 240, 600, 1200 therefore admits 60 and 120 for every candidate, not 240.
   158	- **W2 (polymer floor under the receiver) is unavailable by construction**: ordinary placement requires empty cells and W2 puts polymer on every footprint cell (0 common placeable cells; the probe is part of the evidence). W1 (ring around the footprint) is the only ordinary-placement geometry and is the one control.
   159	- **W1 on dev (8 wall seeds, four gating contrasts including inoc2)**: no dose on the ladder passes. Seeds positive per contrast: dose 60: 4/8 on builder, poly, inoc2; 4/8 on space; dose 120: 4/8, 4/8, 6/8 (space median +27.5), 4/8; dose 240: 5/8, 5/8, 7/8 (space median +29.5), 5/8; dose 600 and 1200 are worse than the comparators (medians about -172 and -190). So the minimum detectable dose does not exist on this ladder (up to 1200, about 6 times the ceiling), the noise gate cannot be met either (it needs the weakest contrast median to be at least twice delta, i.e. above about 2,300; the best observed is about 30), and **all three candidates are `unsupported-measurement` with the stated reason that no ordinary-placement wall is detectable at any tested dose**. No fresh block is simulated; `--write` seals a stopped payload (fixtures and the frozen calibration only).
   160	- Seeds: block, replicate (primary and noise offsets), wall and fixture seeds are enumerated in the protocol (the lock lists every seed that can run: 8,601,001-8,608,999 only); never 8600101-108 or 8600501-508. Fresh seeds 8601501-516 are listed in the protocol and never read, because no candidate qualified.
   161	- Registry binding: this rung digests its own single reservation line (owner `i06b free-energy-matched`); the first rung's `LATER_SLICE_OWNERS` skips it, so neither rung's evidence depends on the other's reservation. The first rung's manifest.json is re-pinned (source hashes only).
   162	- Dev contrasts for the record: `inoc2` was negative in 24 of 24 dev blocks (as `inoc` was), so the free-energy-matched comparators did not rescue a benefit; the free-energy gap is real (receiver-alone holds about 19,900 free E against the field arm's about 9,400 at equal total) and is closed by fU/fS/fB exactly.
   163	
   164	Fresh seeds are read only after this amendment, the lock, the calibration and the sources are committed and pushed (`freezeGuard`); with no qualifying candidate the fresh seeds are never read at all.
   165	
   166	## 11. Non-goals and limits
   167	
   168	- Not a new organism, predation, or mutation test; one receiver genome, one field per candidate, T1 = 60, T2 = 80, 24x24, single tile.
   169	- Free energy cannot be matched together with total energy at equal matter; reporting both families and the signed `constructionGap` is the honest alternative. A pass shows no more than the power statement in 4.2 allows.
   170	- The positive control is a hand-built wall under ordinary placement; it shows the assay can detect a polymer structure of realistic dose, not that a builder's field can pass. The builder founder-biomass dose-response and a polymer-bearing full-gate trap fixture are cut from this rung and left to a later one. A failed control is a finding about assay sensitivity, not about niche construction.
   171	- Sum-of-R readout reduces replicate noise, not block-to-block field variance; the block count carries that.
   172	- No GPU evidence, no deploy, no edits to frozen preregistration, rung-1 evidence stays sealed and unedited (rung 1's own conclusion is superseded only by a rung-2 label, not rewritten).
   173	- Not part of the frozen preregistration; exploratory.
   174

    18	// The first rung's CLI dispatches here while its own top-level await is still pending, so this module must not import it statically
    19	// (a cycle would never settle). mainFe receives the helpers; tests and other callers get them lazily.
    20	type Helpers = Pick<typeof NicheTool, "sha256" | "registerAliases" | "sourceFiles">;
    21	let helperModule: Helpers | undefined;
    22	const helpers = async (): Promise<Helpers> => (helperModule ??= await import("./niche.ts"));
    23	export const REPO = fileURLToPath(new URL("../../", import.meta.url));
    24	const serialize = (p: unknown): string => `${JSON.stringify(p, null, 1)}\n`;
    25	const sha256 = async (text: string | Uint8Array): Promise<string> => (await helpers()).sha256(text);

    55	/** Digest over this rung's own reservation line(s) only: another slice's reservation never invalidates this evidence. */
    56	export const registryDigestFe = async (root = REPO): Promise<string> => {
    57	  const text = readFileSync(`${root}${REGISTRY_REL}`, "utf8");
    58	  const own = text.split("\n").filter((line) => line.includes(`"owner": "${OWNER}`));
    59	  if (own.length !== 1) throw new Error(`expected exactly one registry reservation owned by "${OWNER}", found ${own.length}`);
    60	  return sha256(own.join("\n"));
    61	};

    63	/** Every file whose bytes feed this rung's evidence: the first rung's source set plus this tool. */
    64	export const frozenSources = async (root = REPO): Promise<string[]> => [...(await helpers()).sourceFiles(root), "tools/niche/fe.ts"];
    65	export const hashSources = async (root = REPO): Promise<Record<string, string>> => {
    66	  const out: Record<string, string> = {};
    67	  for (const f of await frozenSources(root)) out[f] = await sha256(readFileSync(root + f));
    68	  return out;
    69	};

    71	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    72	// Freeze-before-run guard
    73	
    74	export type Git = (args: string[]) => string;
    75	export const realGit: Git = (args) => execFileSync("git", args, { cwd: REPO, encoding: "utf8", maxBuffer: 1 << 26 });
    76	
    77	/** Paths whose bytes the lock binds (or that the lock itself is): they must be committed and identical on the pushed branch. */
    78	export const frozenPaths = async (root = REPO): Promise<string[]> => [...(await frozenSources(root)), REGISTRY_REL, rel(CALIBRATION_FE), rel(LOCK_FE)];
    79	
    80	/**
    81	 * Refuse unless the freeze is clean and pushed: the frozen paths have no uncommitted change, and each path's blob on
    82	 * origin/<BRANCH> equals the blob in the working tree (git ls-tree against git hash-object). The lock cannot name its own commit,
    83	 * so this identical-on-the-pushed-branch check is the ordering proof. Returns problems (empty when the guard allows).
    84	 */
    85	export function freezeGuard(paths: string[], git: Git = realGit, ref = `origin/${BRANCH}`): string[] {
    86	  const problems: string[] = [];
    87	  let status = "";
    88	  try {
    89	    status = git(["status", "--porcelain", "--", ...paths]);
    90	  } catch (e) {
    91	    return [`git status failed: ${(e as Error).message}`];
    92	  }
    93	  for (const line of status.split("\n").filter(Boolean)) problems.push(`frozen file not committed clean: ${line.trim()}`);
    94	  let tree = "";
    95	  try {
    96	    tree = git(["ls-tree", "-r", ref, "--", ...paths]);
    97	  } catch {
    98	    return [...problems, `${ref} is not available: push the freeze commit first (git push -u origin ${BRANCH})`];
    99	  }
   100	  const remote = new Map<string, string>();
   101	  for (const line of tree.split("\n").filter(Boolean)) {
   102	    const m = /^\d+ blob ([0-9a-f]+)\t(.+)$/.exec(line);
   103	    if (m) remote.set(m[2], m[1]);
   104	  }
   105	  const local = git(["hash-object", "--", ...paths]).split("\n").filter(Boolean);
   106	  paths.forEach((path, i) => {
   107	    if (!remote.has(path)) problems.push(`${path} is not on ${ref}`);
   108	    else if (remote.get(path) !== local[i]) problems.push(`${path} differs from ${ref}`);
   109	  });
   110	  return problems;
   111	}
   112	
   113	/** Lock verification: protocol, calibration, seeds, registry digest and every source hash against the current tree. */
   114	export async function verifyLock(): Promise<string[]> {
   115	  const L = await libFe();
   116	  const problems: string[] = [];
   117	  if (!existsSync(LOCK_FE)) return ["no protocol.lock.json: run --calibrate and --freeze before the fresh run"];
   118	  if (!existsSync(CALIBRATION_FE)) return ["no calibration.json"];
   119	  const lock = JSON.parse(readFileSync(LOCK_FE, "utf8"));
   120	  const digest = await registryDigestFe();
   121	  const canon = L.schema.canonicalJSON;
   122	  if (lock.registryDigest !== digest) problems.push("registry reservation changed since the freeze");
   123	  if (lock.protocolHash !== L.protocolHashFe(digest, L.PROTOCOL_FE)) problems.push("the protocol in code differs from protocol.lock.json");
   124	  if (canon(lock.seeds) !== canon(L.enumerateSeedsFe(L.PROTOCOL_FE))) problems.push("lock seed list differs from the protocol");
   125	  if (lock.calibrationSha256 !== (await sha256(readFileSync(CALIBRATION_FE)))) problems.push("calibration.json changed since the freeze");
   126	  const now = await hashSources();
   127	  if (canon(lock.sources) !== canon(now)) for (const f of new Set([...Object.keys(lock.sources ?? {}), ...Object.keys(now)])) if (lock.sources?.[f] !== now[f]) problems.push(`source ${f} differs from the freeze`);
   128	  return problems;
   129	}
   130	
   131	async function guarded(git: Git = realGit): Promise<void> {
   132	  const problems = [...(await verifyLock()), ...freezeGuard(await frozenPaths(), git)];
   133	  if (problems.length) throw new Error(`refusing to read any fresh seed:\n${problems.join("\n")}`);
   134	}
   135	
   136	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   137	
   138	const logger = (t0: number) => (s: string) => console.log(`[${Math.round((Date.now() - t0) / 1000)}s] ${s}`);
   139	
   140	export async function generateFe(): Promise<unknown> {
   141	  const L = await libFe();
   142	  const dev = JSON.parse(readFileSync(CALIBRATION_FE, "utf8"));
   143	  return L.buildPayloadFe(await registryDigestFe(), dev, L.PROTOCOL_FE, logger(Date.now()));
   144	}
   145	
   146	export async function makeManifestFe(payload: any, evidenceText: string, seconds: number): Promise<Record<string, unknown>> {
   147	  return {
   148	    kind: "niche-fe-evidence-manifest",
   149	    payloadFileSha256: await sha256(evidenceText),
   150	    integritySha256: payload.integritySha256,
   151	    protocolHash: payload.protocolHash,
   152	    registryDigest: payload.registryDigest,
   153	    stepProjection: payload.stepProjection,
   154	    sources: await hashSources(),
   155	    provenance: { note: "not replayed; excluded from the deterministic payload", node: process.version, platform: `${platform()}-${arch()}`, regenerationSeconds: Math.round(seconds), generatedAt: new Date().toISOString() },
   156	  };
   157	}
   158	
   159	/** Validate saved artifacts without simulating. Returns problems (empty when sound). */
   160	export async function checkSavedFe(root = REPO): Promise<string[]> {
   161	  const L = await libFe();
   162	  const problems: string[] = [];
   163	  const paths = { evidence: EVIDENCE_FE, manifest: MANIFEST_FE, calibration: CALIBRATION_FE, lock: LOCK_FE };
   164	  for (const f of Object.values(paths)) if (!existsSync(f)) problems.push(`${f} is missing`);
   165	  if (problems.length) return problems;
   166	  const text = readFileSync(paths.evidence, "utf8");
   167	  const payload = JSON.parse(text);
   168	  const manifest = JSON.parse(readFileSync(paths.manifest, "utf8"));
   169	  const lock = JSON.parse(readFileSync(paths.lock, "utf8"));
   170	  const calibration = JSON.parse(readFileSync(paths.calibration, "utf8"));
   171	  const canon = L.schema.canonicalJSON;
   172	  if (!L.verifySealFe(payload)) problems.push("integritySha256 does not match the payload");
   173	  if (manifest.payloadFileSha256 !== (await sha256(text))) problems.push("manifest payloadFileSha256 does not match evidence.json");
   174	  if (manifest.integritySha256 !== payload.integritySha256) problems.push("manifest seal differs from the payload seal");
   175	  if (manifest.protocolHash !== payload.protocolHash) problems.push("manifest protocolHash differs from the payload");
   176	  const digest = await registryDigestFe(root);
   177	  if (payload.registryDigest !== digest) problems.push("this rung's registry reservation changed since the evidence was written");
   178	  if (lock.protocolHash !== payload.protocolHash) problems.push("protocol.lock.json does not match the payload protocol hash");
   179	  if (lock.protocolHash !== L.protocolHashFe(digest, L.PROTOCOL_FE)) problems.push("the protocol in code differs from the frozen lock");
   180	  if (canon(payload.protocol) !== canon(L.PROTOCOL_FE)) problems.push("payload protocol differs from the protocol in code");
   181	  if (canon(lock.seeds) !== canon(L.enumerateSeedsFe(L.PROTOCOL_FE))) problems.push("lock seed list differs from the protocol");
   182	  if (canon(calibration) !== canon(payload.dev)) problems.push("calibration.json differs from the dev section of evidence.json");
   183	  if (lock.calibrationSha256 !== (await sha256(readFileSync(paths.calibration)))) problems.push("lock calibrationSha256 does not match calibration.json");
   184	  for (const c of payload.dev.candidates) if (L.PROTOCOL_FE.delta[String(c.cluster)] !== c.deltaProposed) problems.push(`delta for cluster ${c.cluster} is not the dev replicate-noise floor`);
   185	  // Seed hygiene: every seed that can run lies in this rung's reservation, never in another owner's (rung 1's seeds included).
   186	  const registry = JSON.parse(readFileSync(`${root}${REGISTRY_REL}`, "utf8")).reservations as { first: number; last: number; owner: string }[];
   187	  const all = L.enumerateSeedsFe(L.PROTOCOL_FE).map((seed, i) => ({ id: `s${i}`, seed }));
   188	  for (const f of L.schema.seedCollisions(all, registry.filter((r) => !r.owner.startsWith(OWNER)), OWNER)) problems.push(`seed collision: ${f}`);
   189	  for (const s of all) if (!registry.some((r) => r.owner.startsWith(OWNER) && s.seed >= r.first && s.seed <= r.last)) problems.push(`seed ${s.seed} is outside this rung's reservation`);
   190	  // Decision and qualification recomputation from the stored inputs.
   191	  const recomputed = L.decideCandidatesFe(payload.fresh.inputs, L.PROTOCOL_FE.viableBlocksMin);
   192	  if (canon(recomputed) !== canon(payload.fresh.results)) problems.push("fresh results do not follow the stated decision rules");
   193	  if (canon(L.panelVerdictFe(recomputed)) !== canon(payload.fresh.panel)) problems.push("panel verdict does not follow the stated rules");
   194	  const qual = payload.dev.candidates.map((c: any) => L.qualify(c.cluster, c.deltaProposed, c.builderPMedianTimes2, payload.dev.ladder.rows, L.PROTOCOL_FE));
   195	  if (canon(qual) !== canon(payload.dev.qualification)) problems.push("qualification does not follow the stated positive-control rules");
   196	  for (const q of payload.dev.qualification) {
   197	    const sim = payload.fresh.candidates.some((c: any) => c.cluster === q.cluster);
   198	    if (sim !== q.qualified) problems.push(`cluster ${q.cluster}: ${q.qualified ? "qualified but not simulated" : "not qualified but simulated"}`);
   199	  }
   200	  // Every ledger exactly zero.
   201	  const blocks = [...payload.dev.candidates, ...payload.fresh.candidates].flatMap((c: any) => c.blocks);
   202	  for (const b of blocks) for (const [arm, recs] of Object.entries<any[]>(b.transfers)) for (const r of recs) if (r.residual !== "0" || r.matterResidual !== "0") problems.push(`block ${b.seed} arm ${arm} op ${r.op}: nonzero residual`);
   203	  // Sources.
   204	  const now = await hashSources(root);
   205	  if (canon(manifest.sources) !== canon(now)) for (const f of new Set([...Object.keys(manifest.sources), ...Object.keys(now)])) if (manifest.sources[f] !== now[f]) problems.push(`source ${f} changed since the evidence was written`);
   206	  if (canon(lock.sources) !== canon(now)) problems.push("sources differ from the frozen lock");
   207	  return problems;
   208	}
   209	
   210	export async function replayCheckFe(): Promise<string[]> {
   211	  await guarded();
   212	  const fresh = serialize(await generateFe());
   213	  return fresh === readFileSync(EVIDENCE_FE, "utf8") ? [] : ["regenerated payload differs from evidence.json"];
   214	}
   215	
   216	async function repinRung1(): Promise<void> {
   217	  const m = JSON.parse(readFileSync(RUNG1_MANIFEST, "utf8"));
   218	  const sources: Record<string, string> = {};
   219	  for (const f of (await helpers()).sourceFiles()) sources[f] = await sha256(readFileSync(REPO + f));
   220	  m.sources = sources;
   221	  writeAtomic(RUNG1_MANIFEST, serialize(m));
   222	  console.log(`re-pinned ${Object.keys(sources).length} source hashes in the first rung's manifest.json (nothing else changed)`);
   223	}
   224	
   225	export async function mainFe(argv: string[], injected?: Helpers): Promise<void> {
   226	  if (injected) helperModule = injected;
   227	  const mode = argv[2];
   228	  const L = await libFe();
   229	  const t0 = Date.now();
   230	  const log = logger(t0);
   231	  if (mode === "--bench") {
   232	    const p = L.PROTOCOL_FE;
   233	    const r = L.runBlockFe(p.panel[0], p.devSeeds[0], p);
   234	    const ms = Date.now() - t0;
   235	    const steps = 2 * p.T1 + 13 * p.R * p.T2;
   236	    console.log(`block valid=${r.valid}: ${steps} steps in ${ms} ms = ${(ms / steps).toFixed(2)} ms/step`);
   237	    const proj = L.stepProjectionFe(p);
   238	    console.log(`fresh run: ${proj.freshBlocks} blocks x ${proj.stepsPerBlock} steps ~ ${Math.round((proj.freshBlocks * ms) / 1000)} s; dev blocks ${proj.devBlocks} ~ ${Math.round((proj.devBlocks * ms) / 1000)} s`);
   239	    return;
   240	  }
   241	  if (mode === "--calibrate") {
   242	    const dev = L.devReportFe(L.PROTOCOL_FE, log);
   243	    writeAtomic(CALIBRATION_FE, serialize(dev));
   244	    for (const q of dev.qualification) console.log(`cluster ${q.cluster}: ${q.qualified ? "QUALIFIED" : "not qualified"} delta ${q.delta} ceiling ${q.ceiling} minDetectableDose ${q.minDetectableDose} qualifyingDose ${q.qualifyingDose}: ${q.reason}`);
   245	    console.log(`W2: ${dev.ladder.w2.status} (${dev.ladder.w2.reason})`);
   246	    console.log("wrote calibration.json (dev only). Copy the deltas into PROTOCOL_FE.delta, then --freeze.");
   247	    return;
   248	  }
   249	  if (mode === "--freeze") {
   250	    if (!existsSync(CALIBRATION_FE)) throw new Error("run --calibrate first");
   251	    if (existsSync(LOCK_FE) && !argv.includes("--force")) throw new Error("protocol.lock.json exists; pass --force to rewrite deliberately.");
   252	    const digest = await registryDigestFe();
   253	    const calibration = JSON.parse(readFileSync(CALIBRATION_FE, "utf8"));
   254	    for (const c of calibration.candidates) if (L.PROTOCOL_FE.delta[String(c.cluster)] !== c.deltaProposed) throw new Error(`PROTOCOL_FE.delta[${c.cluster}] must be ${c.deltaProposed} (the dev replicate-noise floor)`);
   255	    if (calibration.R !== L.PROTOCOL_FE.R) throw new Error("calibration was run with a different R");
   256	    const lock = { kind: "niche-fe-protocol-lock", protocolHash: L.protocolHashFe(digest, L.PROTOCOL_FE), registryDigest: digest, seeds: L.enumerateSeedsFe(L.PROTOCOL_FE), calibrationSha256: await sha256(readFileSync(CALIBRATION_FE)), sources: await hashSources(), note: "written before the fresh-seed run; fresh results never tune the protocol", frozenAt: new Date().toISOString() };
   257	    writeAtomic(LOCK_FE, serialize(lock));
   258	    console.log(`wrote protocol.lock.json ${lock.protocolHash}`);
   259	    return;
   260	  }
   261	  if (mode === "--write") {
   262	    if (existsSync(EVIDENCE_FE) && !argv.includes("--force")) throw new Error("evidence.json exists; files are written once. Pass --force to rewrite deliberately.");
   263	    await guarded();
   264	    const payload = (await generateFe()) as any;
   265	    const seconds = (Date.now() - t0) / 1000;
   266	    const text = serialize(payload);
   267	    const manifestText = serialize(await makeManifestFe(payload, text, seconds));
   268	    writeFileSync(`${EVIDENCE_FE}.tmp`, text);
   269	    writeFileSync(`${MANIFEST_FE}.tmp`, manifestText);
   270	    renameSync(`${EVIDENCE_FE}.tmp`, EVIDENCE_FE);
   271	    renameSync(`${MANIFEST_FE}.tmp`, MANIFEST_FE);
   272	    console.log(`wrote evidence.json (${text.length} bytes) seal ${payload.integritySha256} in ${seconds.toFixed(0)} s; verdict: ${payload.fresh.panel.verdict}`);
   273	    return;
   274	  }
   275	  if (mode === "--check" || mode === "--replay-check") {
   276	    const problems = mode === "--check" ? await checkSavedFe() : await replayCheckFe();
   277	    if (problems.length) {
   278	      console.error(problems.join("\n"));
   279	      process.exit(1);
   280	    }
   281	    console.log(`rung fe ${mode} ok${mode === "--replay-check" ? ` (${Math.round((Date.now() - t0) / 1000)} s)` : ""}`);
   282	    return;
   283	  }
   284	  if (mode === "--repin-rung1") return repinRung1();
   285	  console.error("usage: node tools/niche/niche.ts --rung fe --bench | --calibrate | --freeze | --write | --check | --replay-check | --repin-rung1");
   286	  process.exit(2);
   287	}

     1	// I06b evidence assembly: dev (calibration) report, fresh (confirmation) report for qualified candidates only, fixtures, guards, the
     2	// decision and the seal. Pure and deterministic; integers, integer rationals and decimal strings only. `--write` and `--replay-check`
     3	// load the frozen calibration (dev) and regenerate only the fresh blocks and the fixtures.
     4	import { canonicalJSON, sha256HexJS } from "@bl/schema";
     5	import { medianTimes2 } from "./decision.ts";
     6	import { decideCandidatesFe, panelVerdictFe, type CandidateInputFe, type CandidateResultFe, type PanelResultFe } from "./decisionFe.ts";
     7	import { deltaOf } from "./evidence.ts";
     8	import { runLadder, qualify, type LadderReport, type Qualification } from "./dose.ts";
     9	import { fieldBlock, fixtureProtocolFe, decideFixtureFe, freeGapSeed, nullBlockFe, runGuardsFe, takeoverBlockFe, wallGateBlockFe2, type FieldVariant, type FixtureReportFe, type FreeGapSeed, type GuardFe } from "./fixturesFe.ts";
    10	import { GATING_FE, PROTOCOL_FE, enumerateSeedsFe, protocolHashFe, type ProtocolFe } from "./protocol.ts";

    13	export const EVIDENCE_VERSION_FE = "niche-fe-evidence-v1";
    14	
    15	export interface DevCandidateFe {
    16	  cluster: number;
    17	  blocks: BlockResultFe[];
    18	  noise: { absBuilder: number[]; absNonbuilder: number[] };
    19	  deltaProposed: number;
    20	  /** Twice the median over dev blocks of the builder history's total polymer at the end of conditioning (also the realistic ceiling 2 x median). */
    21	  builderPMedianTimes2: number;
    22	}
    23	
    24	export interface DevReportFe {
    25	  purpose: "calibration";
    26	  R: number;
    27	  seeds: readonly number[];
    28	  candidates: DevCandidateFe[];
    29	  ladder: LadderReport;
    30	  qualification: Qualification[];
    31	}
    32	
    33	export function devReportFe(p: ProtocolFe = PROTOCOL_FE, log: (s: string) => void = () => {}): DevReportFe {
    34	  const candidates: DevCandidateFe[] = p.panel.map((cluster) => {
    35	    const blocks = p.devSeeds.map((s) => runBlockFe(cluster, s, p));
    36	    const invalid = blocks.filter((b) => !b.valid);
    37	    if (invalid.length) throw new Error(`cluster ${cluster}: invalid dev block(s) ${invalid.map((b) => `${b.seed} (${b.invalidReason})`).join(", ")}`);
    38	    const noiseBlocks = p.devReplicateSeeds.map((s) => noiseBlock(cluster, s, p));
    39	    const noise = { absBuilder: noiseBlocks.map((n) => n.absBuilder), absNonbuilder: noiseBlocks.map((n) => n.absNonbuilder) };
    40	    const pMed2 = medianTimes2(blocks.map((b) => Number(b.costs.find((c) => c.kind === "builder")?.P ?? 0)));
    41	    const deltaProposed = deltaOf(p.reserve.B * p.R, [...noise.absBuilder, ...noise.absNonbuilder]);
    42	    log(`cluster ${cluster}: delta ${deltaProposed}, builder P median x2 ${pMed2}`);
    43	    return { cluster, blocks, noise, deltaProposed, builderPMedianTimes2: pMed2 };
    44	  });
    45	  const deltas = Object.fromEntries(candidates.map((c) => [String(c.cluster), c.deltaProposed]));
    46	  const ladder = runLadder(deltas, p, (row) => log(`dose ${row.totalP}: ${GATING_FE.map((k) => `${k} ${row.stats[k].k}/${p.wallSeeds.length} med ${row.stats[k].medianTimes2 / 2}`).join(", ")}`));
    47	  const qualification = candidates.map((c) => qualify(c.cluster, c.deltaProposed, c.builderPMedianTimes2, ladder.rows, p));
    48	  return { purpose: "calibration", R: p.R, seeds: p.devSeeds, candidates, ladder, qualification };
    49	}
    50	
    51	export interface FreshCandidateFe {
    52	  cluster: number;
    53	  blocks: BlockResultFe[];
    54	  input: CandidateInputFe;
    55	  /** Per arm: twice the median of Y (sum over replicates) over the fresh blocks. */
    56	  medianYTimes2: Record<string, number | null>;
    57	  survivedByArm: Record<string, { reps: number; of: number }>;
    58	  /** Paired survival contrast: blocks where the field arm had strictly more surviving replicates than the best comparator (attribution only). */
    59	  survivalPositiveBlocks: number;
    60	  fluxAttribution: { available: boolean; reason: string; growthAttributedBlocks: number; coCulture: "unavailable" };
    61	  /** Secondary, never gating: twice the median of the per-arm AUC. */
    62	  aucMedianTimes2: Record<string, number | null>;
    63	}
    64	
    65	export interface FreshReportFe {
    66	  purpose: "confirmation";
    67	  seeds: readonly number[];
    68	  /** false when no candidate qualified: no fresh seed was simulated. */
    69	  ran: boolean;
    70	  candidates: FreshCandidateFe[];
    71	  inputs: CandidateInputFe[];
    72	  results: CandidateResultFe[];
    73	  panel: PanelResultFe;
    74	  caveats: string[];
    75	}
    76	
    77	const med = (xs: readonly number[]): number | null => (xs.length ? medianTimes2(xs) : null);
    78	
    79	export function freshReportFe(dev: DevReportFe, p: ProtocolFe = PROTOCOL_FE, log: (s: string) => void = () => {}): FreshReportFe {
    80	  const n = p.freshSeeds.length;
    81	  const zeros = Array(n).fill(0);
    82	  const candidates: FreshCandidateFe[] = [];
    83	  const inputs: CandidateInputFe[] = [];
    84	  for (const q of dev.qualification) {
    85	    const delta = p.delta[String(q.cluster)];
    86	    if (!q.qualified) {
    87	      inputs.push({ id: `cluster-${q.cluster}`, status: "not-simulated", invalidReasons: [], contrasts: { builder: zeros, poly: zeros, space: zeros, inoc2: zeros, spaceP: zeros, spaceNP: zeros, inoc: zeros, naive: zeros }, delta, viableBlocks: 0, blocks: n, expectedBlocks: n, notSimulatedReasons: [q.reason] });
    88	      continue;
    89	    }
    90	    const raw = p.freshSeeds.map((s) => runBlockFe(q.cluster, s, p));
    91	    log(`cluster ${q.cluster}: fresh ${raw.filter((b) => b.valid).length}/${n} valid`);
    92	    const col = (k: keyof ContrastsFe) => raw.map((b) => (b.contrasts ? b.contrasts[k] : 0));
    93	    const valid = raw.filter((b) => b.valid).length;
    94	    const input: CandidateInputFe = {
    95	      id: `cluster-${q.cluster}`,
    96	      status: valid === n ? "ok" : "invalid",
    97	      invalidReasons: raw.filter((b) => !b.valid).map((b) => `seed ${b.seed}: ${b.invalidReason}`),
    98	      contrasts: { builder: col("builder"), poly: col("poly"), space: col("space"), inoc2: col("inoc2"), spaceP: col("spaceP"), spaceNP: col("spaceNP"), inoc: col("inoc"), naive: col("naive") },
    99	      delta,
   100	      viableBlocks: raw.filter((b) => b.viable).length,
   101	      blocks: valid,
   102	      expectedBlocks: n,
   103	      notSimulatedReasons: [],
   104	    };
   105	    inputs.push(input);
   106	    const sat = raw.filter((b) => Object.values(b.outcomes).some((o) => o.saturated)).length;
   107	    candidates.push({
   108	      cluster: q.cluster,
   109	      blocks: raw,
   110	      input,
   111	      medianYTimes2: Object.fromEntries(ARMS_FE.map((a) => [a, med(raw.filter((b) => b.outcomes[a]).map((b) => b.outcomes[a].y))])),
   112	      survivedByArm: Object.fromEntries(ARMS_FE.map((a) => [a, { reps: raw.reduce((t, b) => t + (b.outcomes[a]?.survivedReps ?? 0), 0), of: raw.filter((b) => b.outcomes[a]).length * p.R }])),
   113	      survivalPositiveBlocks: raw.filter((b) => (b.contrasts?.survivalVsInoc2 ?? 0) > 0).length,
   114	      fluxAttribution: valid === 0 ? { available: false, reason: "no valid block produced a measurement", growthAttributedBlocks: 0, coCulture: "unavailable" } : sat === 0 ? { available: true, reason: "no role counter reached saturation in any replicate; the co-culture arm (a) is never attributed", growthAttributedBlocks: raw.filter((b) => (b.outcomes.b?.growthAttributedReps ?? 0) * 2 >= p.R).length, coCulture: "unavailable" } : { available: false, reason: `a role counter reached ${p.roleSaturation} in ${sat} block(s)`, growthAttributedBlocks: 0, coCulture: "unavailable" },
   115	      aucMedianTimes2: Object.fromEntries(ARMS_FE.map((a) => [a, med(raw.filter((b) => b.outcomes[a]).map((b) => b.outcomes[a].auc))])),
   116	    });
   117	  }
   118	  const results = decideCandidatesFe(inputs, p.viableBlocksMin);
   119	  const caveats = [p.powerStatement];
   120	  if (candidates.length === 0) caveats.push("No candidate qualified on the dev positive control, so no fresh seed was simulated: this is a statement about the sensitivity of the assay at realistic polymer doses, not about niche construction.");
   121	  return { purpose: "confirmation", seeds: p.freshSeeds, ran: candidates.length > 0, candidates, inputs, results, panel: panelVerdictFe(results), caveats };
   122	}
   123	
   124	export interface FixturesReportFe {
   125	  protocol: { grid: number; kernelRadius: number; T2: number; R: number; reserve: { B: number; E: number }; delta: number };
   126	  seeds: readonly number[];
   127	  reports: FixtureReportFe[];
   128	  freeGap: FreeGapSeed[];
   129	  guards: GuardFe[];
   130	  /** Rung-1 W2 wall (allowP) at the frozen scale through the second-rung arms; diagnostic, never qualifying. */
   131	  handWallGate: FixtureReportFe;
   132	}
   133	
   134	export function fixturesReportFe(p: ProtocolFe = PROTOCOL_FE): FixturesReportFe {
   135	  const fp = fixtureProtocolFe(p);
   136	  const seeds = p.fixtureSeeds;
   137	  const delta = Math.ceil((fp.reserve.B * fp.R) / 50);
   138	  const reports: FixtureReportFe[] = [];
   139	  for (const v of ["far-field", "uniform", "footprint"] as FieldVariant[]) reports.push(decideFixtureFe(v === "far-field" ? "far-field-energy" : `energy-${v}`, seeds, seeds.map((s) => fieldBlock(v, s, fp)), delta));
   140	  reports.push(decideFixtureFe("takeover-only", seeds, seeds.map((s) => takeoverBlockFe(s, fp)), delta));
   141	  reports.push(decideFixtureFe("null-field", seeds, seeds.map((s) => nullBlockFe(s, fp)), delta));
   142	  const wp: ProtocolFe = { ...p, R: p.fixtureR };
   143	  const handWallGate = decideFixtureFe("hand-wall-gate-W2", seeds, seeds.map((s) => wallGateBlockFe2(s, wp)), Math.ceil((p.reserve.B * p.fixtureR) / 50));
   144	  return { protocol: { grid: fp.grid, kernelRadius: fp.kernelRadius, T2: fp.T2, R: fp.R, reserve: fp.reserve, delta }, seeds, reports, freeGap: seeds.map((s) => freeGapSeed(s, fp)), guards: runGuardsFe(fp), handWallGate };
   145	}
   146	
   147	export function stepProjectionFe(p: ProtocolFe = PROTOCOL_FE): { formula: string; freshBlocks: number; stepsPerBlock: number; devBlocks: number } {
   148	  const perBlock = 2 * p.T1 + 13 * p.R * p.T2;
   149	  return { formula: "per block 2*T1 + 13 arms * R * T2; dev blocks, replicate-noise, wall ladder (12 arms * R * T2 per wall seed per dose) and fixtures are calibration or unit scale", freshBlocks: p.panel.length * p.freshSeeds.length, stepsPerBlock: perBlock, devBlocks: p.panel.length * p.devSeeds.length };
   150	}

   187	  return { ...body, integritySha256: sha256HexJS(new TextEncoder().encode(canonicalJSON(body))) };

   190	  const { integritySha256, ...body } = p;

   194	/** Build the sealed payload around a FROZEN dev report (calibration.json): only the fixtures and the fresh blocks are simulated here. */
   195	export function buildPayloadFe(registryDigest: string, dev: DevReportFe, p: ProtocolFe = PROTOCOL_FE, log: (s: string) => void = () => {}): PayloadFe {
   196	  const fresh = freshReportFe(dev, p, log);

     1	// I06b positive control at realistic scale (PLAN section 4.3): a hand-built polymer wall under the ORDINARY placement rule (no
     2	// overwrite of polymer; same pool-cap and reserve guards), dose ladder ascending, the four gating contrasts per wall seed.
     3	// W1 (ring around the footprint) is the only geometry that placement can legally accommodate; W2 (floor under the receiver) needs
     4	// allowP and is probed here only to record why it is unavailable.
     5	import { receiverAlone, placeAll } from "./arms.ts";
     6	import { medianTimes2, signTestP } from "./decision.ts";
     7	import { buildFreeMatched } from "./freeEnergy.ts";
     8	import { buildWall, controlsAround, nominalBudget, wallCells } from "./fixtures.ts";
     9	import { GATING_FE, PROTOCOL_FE, type GatingFe, type ProtocolFe } from "./protocol.ts";
    10	import { contrastsOfFe, runArmReplicated, type ArmOutcomeFe } from "./replicate.ts";
    11	import { NicheInvalid, cloneAudit, cloneState } from "./transfer.ts";
    12	import type { PreArm } from "./arms.ts";
    13	
    14	export interface WallSeedResult {
    15	  seed: number;
    16	  contrasts: Record<GatingFe, number>;
    17	  /** Y of the wall arm b (summed over replicates) and receiver-present replicates. */
    18	  yWall: number;
    19	  viableReps: number;
    20	}
    21	
    22	/** One wall seed at one dose: wall W1 through every arm and replicate under ordinary placement. */
    23	export function wallGateBlockFe(seed: number, totalP: number, p: ProtocolFe = PROTOCOL_FE): WallSeedResult {
    24	  const pp: ProtocolFe = { ...p, wall: { ...p.wall, totalP } };
    25	  const base = receiverAlone({ audit: nominalBudget(seed, pp) }, seed, pp);
    26	  const wall: PreArm = { id: "b", state: cloneState(base.state), audit: cloneAudit(base.audit), records: [] };
    27	  buildWall(wall, wallCells("W1", pp), totalP, pp);
    28	  const arms = [...controlsAround(wall, base, seed, pp)];
    29	  const fm = buildFreeMatched(arms[0], pp);
    30	  if (fm.arms.length === 0) throw new NicheInvalid("every free-energy-matched comparator was refused");
    31	  arms.push(...fm.arms);
    32	  placeAll(arms, pp, { allowP: false });
    33	  const out: Record<string, ArmOutcomeFe> = {};
    34	  for (const x of arms) out[x.id] = runArmReplicated(x, seed, pp, pp.R);
    35	  const c = contrastsOfFe(out);
    36	  return { seed, contrasts: { builder: c.builder, poly: c.poly, space: c.space, inoc2: c.inoc2 }, yWall: out.b.y, viableReps: out.b.survivedReps };
    37	}
    38	
    39	export interface DoseRow {
    40	  totalP: number;
    41	  seeds: readonly number[];
    42	  results: WallSeedResult[];
    43	  /** Per gating contrast: seeds positive and twice the median difference. */
    44	  stats: Record<GatingFe, { k: number; p: { num: number; den: number }; medianTimes2: number }>;
    45	  /** The gating contrast with the smallest median effect: where G-NOISE is read. */
    46	  limiting: GatingFe;
    47	  limitingMedianTimes2: number;
    48	}
    49	
    50	export function doseRow(totalP: number, results: WallSeedResult[]): DoseRow {
    51	  const stats = Object.fromEntries(
    52	    GATING_FE.map((k) => {
    53	      const d = results.map((r) => r.contrasts[k]);
    54	      const kk = d.filter((x) => x > 0).length;
    55	      return [k, { k: kk, p: signTestP(d.length, kk), medianTimes2: medianTimes2(d) }];
    56	    }),
    57	  ) as DoseRow["stats"];
    58	  const limiting = GATING_FE.reduce((m, k) => (stats[k].medianTimes2 < stats[m].medianTimes2 ? k : m), GATING_FE[0]);
    59	  return { totalP, seeds: results.map((r) => r.seed), results, stats, limiting, limitingMedianTimes2: stats[limiting].medianTimes2 };
    60	}
    61	
    62	/** Wall pass at a dose for a candidate: every gating contrast positive in at least wallPassMin seeds and median >= delta. */
    63	export function wallPasses(row: DoseRow, delta: number, p: ProtocolFe = PROTOCOL_FE): boolean {
    64	  return GATING_FE.every((k) => row.stats[k].k >= p.wallPassMin && row.stats[k].medianTimes2 >= 2 * delta);
    65	}
    66	/** G-NOISE: delta at most 1/noiseDivisor of the limiting contrast's median effect (twice-median integers: medianTimes2 >= 2 * noiseDivisor * delta). */
    67	export function noiseGate(row: DoseRow, delta: number, p: ProtocolFe = PROTOCOL_FE): boolean {
    68	  return row.limitingMedianTimes2 >= 2 * p.noiseDivisor * delta;
    69	}
    70	
    71	export interface Qualification {
    72	  cluster: number;
    73	  delta: number;
    74	  /** 2 x the median dev builder polymer at T1 = twice-the-median integer builderPMedianTimes2. */
    75	  ceiling: number;
    76	  /** Smallest dose at which the wall passes ignoring the ceiling (the minimum detectable dose), or null if none on the ladder. */
    77	  minDetectableDose: number | null;
    78	  /** Smallest dose <= ceiling where the wall passes and G-NOISE holds, or null. */
    79	  qualifyingDose: number | null;
    80	  /** Doses <= ceiling on the ladder. */
    81	  dosesWithinCeiling: number[];
    82	  qualified: boolean;
    83	  reason: string;
    84	}
    85	
    86	export function qualify(cluster: number, delta: number, builderPMedianTimes2: number, rows: readonly DoseRow[], p: ProtocolFe = PROTOCOL_FE): Qualification {
    87	  const ceiling = builderPMedianTimes2;
    88	  const within = rows.filter((r) => r.totalP <= ceiling);
    89	  const mdd = rows.find((r) => wallPasses(r, delta, p)) ?? null;
    90	  const q = within.find((r) => wallPasses(r, delta, p) && noiseGate(r, delta, p)) ?? null;
    91	  const reason = q
    92	    ? `W1 passes at ${q.totalP} (<= ceiling ${ceiling}) and delta ${delta} is at most half of the limiting contrast (${q.limiting}) median effect`
    93	    : mdd === null
    94	      ? `W1 is not detected at any ladder dose up to ${rows.length ? rows[rows.length - 1].totalP : 0} (passing needs ${p.wallPassMin} of ${p.wallSeeds.length} seeds positive on all four contrasts and median >= delta ${delta})`
    95	      : mdd.totalP > ceiling
    96	        ? `minimum detectable dose ${mdd.totalP} exceeds the realistic ceiling ${ceiling} (2 x dev builder polymer median)`
    97	        : `W1 passes at ${mdd.totalP} but delta ${delta} exceeds half of the limiting contrast (${mdd.limiting}) median effect ${mdd.limitingMedianTimes2 / 2} (G-NOISE)`;
    98	  return { cluster, delta, ceiling, minDetectableDose: mdd?.totalP ?? null, qualifyingDose: q?.totalP ?? null, dosesWithinCeiling: within.map((r) => r.totalP), qualified: q !== null, reason };
    99	}
   100	
   101	export interface LadderReport {
   102	  geometry: "W1";
   103	  rows: DoseRow[];
   104	  w2: { status: "unavailable"; reason: string } | { status: "available"; reason: string };
   105	}
   106	
   107	/** Probe W2 (polymer floor under the receiver) under ordinary placement: expected to refuse, which is recorded as the reason it is unavailable. */
   108	export function probeW2(p: ProtocolFe = PROTOCOL_FE): LadderReport["w2"] {
   109	  const seed = p.wallSeeds[0];
   110	  const pp: ProtocolFe = { ...p, wall: { ...p.wall, totalP: p.doses[0] } };
   111	  const base = receiverAlone({ audit: nominalBudget(seed, pp) }, seed, pp);
   112	  const wall: PreArm = { id: "b", state: cloneState(base.state), audit: cloneAudit(base.audit), records: [] };
   113	  buildWall(wall, wallCells("W2", pp), p.doses[0], pp);
   114	  try {
   115	    placeAll([wall, base], pp, { allowP: false });
   116	    return { status: "available", reason: "ordinary placement found enough common cells over the floor" };
   117	  } catch (e) {
   118	    if (e instanceof NicheInvalid) return { status: "unavailable", reason: `ordinary placement refuses a polymer floor under the receiver: ${e.reason}` };
   119	    throw e;
   120	  }
   121	}
   122	
   123	/**
   124	 * Run the ladder ascending, stopping once every candidate has a passing dose (its minimum detectable dose is then known);
   125	 * otherwise continue to the top of the ladder to state the minimum detectable dose or its absence.
   126	 */
   127	export function runLadder(deltas: Readonly<Record<string, number>>, p: ProtocolFe = PROTOCOL_FE, onDose?: (row: DoseRow) => void): LadderReport {
   128	  const rows: DoseRow[] = [];
   129	  for (const dose of p.doses) {
   130	    const row = doseRow(dose, p.wallSeeds.map((s) => wallGateBlockFe(s, dose, p)));
   131	    rows.push(row);
   132	    onDose?.(row);
   133	    if (Object.values(deltas).every((d) => rows.some((r) => wallPasses(r, d, p)))) break;
   134	  }
   135	  return { geometry: "W1", rows, w2: probeW2(p) };
   136	}

     1	// I06b replicated Stage 2 (PLAN section 4.2): every arm's Stage 2 is run for R sim seeds (block seed + k * stride), the same
     2	// offsets in every arm, so injury steps and RNG keys coincide across arms. Stage 1 histories and transfers are shared.

    10	export const ARMS_FE = ["a", "b", "c", "cP", "cNP", "nb", "ab", "d0", "d1E", "d1B", "fU", "fS", "fB"] as const;
    11	export type ArmIdFe = (typeof ARMS_FE)[number] | string;
